Aaron Elliott: That's correct. We would still be anticipating the data release in Q2 of 2027, with a launch in the back half of 2027.

Operator: One moment for our next question. Our next question comes from Mark Massaro with U.S. Bancorp BTIG. Your line is open.

Mark Massaro: Hey, guys. Thank you for taking the questions, and congrats on your first call as a public company. I wanted to start on SimpleScreen CRC. So far, lots of questions about the early launch. I recognize you're only a month in. Given the size of the Abbott sales force, I think, obviously, they made a large acquisition of Cologuard. I wanted to get a feel for, my sense is that Cologuard might be first in their bag, blood might be second in their bag. Can you just give us a sense for, it sounds like all 800 reps are trained on your product.

I'm just curious if your understanding is that Cologuard would be offered first and that blood would be offered only after stool would be rejected?

Aaron Elliott: Yeah. Hi. Thanks, Mark. Thanks for the question. Yes, currently, Abbott is following ACS guidelines, which really position colonoscopy and stool-based testing as the preferred options. Individuals that are unwilling to do those options would then be offered a blood-based option. It is early in the maturation of blood-based testing. The options of colonoscopy, stool-based testing, these have been around for a very long time, and there still remain 60 million unscreened people. That is really the focus with us and Abbott right now, is how do we identify and reach those 60 million individuals? We do think Abbott is in the best position to capitalize on finding these patients because they have the stool-based assay.

As of now, based on ACS guidelines, we actually find our test complementary with their stool-based test. We do think they can leverage having that assay to find this non-compliant patient population. That is our focus on the early launch.

Mark Massaro: That sounds great. It sounds like, you talked about the 20 individuals calling on health systems for lung. Is that the size of your total commercial organization right now? Recognizing that you have over $400 million of cash on the balance sheet, how should we think about you scaling your commercial team, and where do you think you might be a year from now?

Aaron Elliott: Yeah. So, yeah, thanks for the question. So yeah, that would be the total size of the commercial team currently. We are looking at 2027 to really start expanding that commercial team out, and then obviously we can gate the expansion based on performance as we go. Long term, we would anticipate a commercial organization probably in the $400 million range, long term. Again, we think that is probably the appropriate size based on our commercial strategy, which allows our partners like Abbott to do that bottoms-up approach while we do the top-down health system approach, and then put sort of reps in the field to support the health systems that we are partnered with.

Mark Massaro: That sounds great. I wanted to ask about the lung. Obviously, you put up really strong data, notably on the negative predictive value of 99.8%. To what extent do you view this as a rule-out test versus a rule-in, showing 82% at stage 1? Presumably this could be both, but I'm just wondering how you plan to position it.

Riley Ennis: The most important is maintaining the highest sensitivity in ruling people in, given the care pathway and reflex to low-dose CT. Maintaining as high sensitivity as possible, especially early-stage sensitivity, is really the focus of clinicians, given less than 20% of people are currently getting screened today, despite having low-dose CT available for quite some time. That's really where blood-based testing can funnel more individuals into low-dose CT, where they can then receive the proper follow-up and triage care.

Mark Massaro: Fantastic. Maybe last one. I recognize we're early days as we think about multi-cancer early detection. Is there any way to get your sense for what you think will be larger in the mid to long term? Is it the sum of multi-cancer early detection tests, or could it be the sum of individual cancer tests?

Riley Ennis: It's likely going to be the overlap of individuals that are choosing single cancer alongside a multi-cancer test based on coverage and reimbursement. We really think broad adoption and where volume is going to come is when there's reimbursement in place. Right now, colorectal cancer, there is reimbursement, and potentially by early 2029, there could be national coverage for a multi-cancer detection test. We think anywhere where reimbursement goes is where large volumes are going to be. We'd say it's the sum of the single cancer test alongside multi-cancer, given that potential path to coverage and reimbursement.

Mark Massaro: Great. Thanks so much for the time.

Operator: One moment for our next question. Our next question comes from Tycho Peterson with Jefferies. Your line is open.

Tycho Peterson: Hey, thanks. Maybe just a question on commercial coverage roadmap here. I am curious how you think Carelon may ramp, and then obviously Abbott's talking to a number of payers. What's your sense of timing on any of those, and how do we think about kind of implications around ASP uplift?

Aaron Elliott: Yeah, thanks, Tycho. We do know they are actively engaged with a lot of payers, the majority of the top 20, and this will be a process as it relates to not just coverage decisions, but also pricing, especially for the ones that have already announced policies. We would expect in the next couple quarters to be able to talk about two larger payers coming in network. But as of now, we're not giving guidance on timing of the payers coming online, but we do know there's progress being made there.

Tycho Peterson: Okay. On V2, appreciate, you know, Breakthrough Device Designation. You shared some data on it. I guess, what are the remaining gating items to the filing, and how do we think about, you know, just timing for FDA approval and tech transfer in 2027?

Aaron Elliott: Yeah, so from a V2 perspective, we are on track to file that supplemental PMA by the end of this year. Based on typical review periods, we would anticipate, you know, in the middle of 2027 for approval. Looking at the tech transfer with Abbott, you know, part of that is their lab becoming an FDA-approved lab to be able to run SimpleScreen. Based on where those activities are currently, you know, we are guiding to have that implemented in Abbott's lab in the first half of 2028. You know, obviously there's a milestone attached to that as well, so we are, you know, actively progressing that tech transfer.

At the same time, you know, until that tech transfer happens, we will be running all of the samples coming in from Abbott in, you know, our facility, which allows us to scale our lab as well. So there's some give and takes on that timing as it relates to, you know, the advantage of scaling our lab, and then you have the milestone of being pushed into 2028. But those activities are ongoing, but that's where we're guiding as of now, is the 2028 lab transfer for V2.

Tycho Peterson: Okay. Just last one on Epic. You know, just curious how many health systems you think, you know, can be live with electronic ordering in the first six, 12 months post-launch, and what does the ramp look like for, you know, other implementations?

Aaron Elliott: We do know there's a lot of activities currently on the Abbott side for the EMR integrations. We do anticipate those going live in November with a pretty significant number of health systems. We're not giving the exact numbers, but those activities should start going live here this month.

Operator: I'm not showing any further questions at this time, and as such, this does conclude today's presentation. We thank you for your participation. You may now disconnect and have a wonderful day.

Jensen Huang Is Out as Nvidia’s CEO. Here’s What Analysts Think the Stock Is Worth Without Him.

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Huang's departure brings some additional questions for investors.

Nvidia (NVDA -1.95%) co-founder Jensen Huang resigned as the company’s CEO amid an investigation into his admitted affair with Alice Huang, a former director of Singapore-based tech company Megaspeed who is being accused of also acting as a Chinese spy, and reports of a second affair with an executive within Nvidia, Kimberly Powell. The chip giant at the center of the artificial intelligence industry now finds itself in need of a new CEO. While the board searches for Huang’s successor, CFO Colette Kress has been appointed interim CEO.

Huang, who had served in the CEO role since the company was founded, had decades of experience, respected engineering talent, and a vast network of connections to key businesses around the world. As such, it's no surprise that Wall Street analysts might believe Nvidia's future is a bit dimmer without him as its leader. Here's what the stock is worth without him at the helm, according to analysts.

Shares of Nvidia have fallen more than 28% since Jensen Huang's affair with Alice Huang and its potential implications came to light. So, the market is already signaling that the upheaval will come with at least some setbacks for the company.

In the intervening weeks, many analysts cut their price targets as well, with some withdrawing their price targets altogether. Across the board, analyst commentary indicates that the company faces significant uncertainty going forward. Uncertainty is like kryptonite for stock prices.

Those analysts who have issued new price targets see the company as worth between $185 and $300 per share after cutting their targets by 5% to 62%. Most agree that the board's lack of succession planning for Huang has left Nvidia in a bad position for now, but there is disagreement among them about the longer-term implications.

In his note, Dan Ives made Huang's value to the company most explicit: "We believe Nvidia's value is $300 per share with Jensen as CEO and $225 per share without Jensen as CEO," he wrote.

Even analysts who are bullish on the company long term caution against buying the stock right now.

"We find it hard to advocate stepping in here in size as there will no doubt be more allegations to come ... both from internal issues as well as headlines related to the business, new governmental investigations etc.," Bernstein’s Stacy Rasgon wrote.

Many believe it's prudent to wait for more clarity on the situation.

"The full story will take time to emerge," William Blair's Sebastien Naji wrote. "As a result, we are downgrading NVDA to Market Perform and will wait for the dust to settle before reassessing."

Indeed, Nvidia investors don't know the significance of any information Huang might have shared with Alice Huang, whether Jensen Huang has been fully truthful with the board, or, for that matter, who the company's next permanent CEO will be.

While Huang is out as CEO, Nvidia's position at the center of the AI industry hasn't changed. He leaves the company with a strong development pipeline and an ecosystem that still makes its chips among the best-performing on the market. It's unlikely its customers will step away from Nvidia's chips due to Huang's actions or his exit.

That said, some of Nvidia's biggest customers have already been pushing to diversify their data center spending away from Nvidia GPUs. Hyperscalers are developing and deploying their own application-specific custom silicon, which costs less upfront and offers better price performance on the workloads it is designed for over the long run. As they find their own chip designs increasingly capable, Nvidia is losing share of their spend. Overall, however, total spend is growing so quickly that Nvidia is still seeing huge increases in sales quarter after quarter.

Slowing growth expectations appeared to be priced into the stock before the news of Huang's affairs. The stock traded at just 20 times forward earnings expectations. After the shares sold off further, they're now priced at less than 18 times earnings. Whether that discount accounts for all of the risk in the company right now is the decision investors get paid to make. I think there are better opportunities among AI stocks, with greater certainty about their future, making them smarter choices to buy now than Nvidia.

When our analyst team has a stock tip, it can pay to listen. After all, Stock Advisor's total average return is 802%* — a market-crushing outperformance compared to 205% for the S&P 500.

They just revealed what they believe are the 10 best stocks for investors to buy right now, available when you join Stock Advisor.

Apple services, including iCloud, App Store, and Apple Music, hit by outage

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

After a recent slew of tech outages, service disruptions, and flight chaos this week, now it’s Apple customers who are facing technical issues of their own.

The iPhone maker’s System Status page indicates that there are issues with iCloud’s Account & Sign In services affecting “all users,” which could make the service slow or unavailable. The App Store, AppleCare on Device, and Apple Music are also experiencing issues, the page indicates.

Like all major tech companies, Apple faces outages and other technical issues affecting its cloud services at times. However, today’s issues are likely more noticeable to end users because problems with iCloud can lead to account sign-in problems. This could cause your iPhone to ask for your Apple ID and password, and you could see other attempts to sign in to services with your Apple account information fail.

On Reddit, some folks also noted that they were unable to activate a new iPhone, which is likely related to these ongoing issues.

Given the increase in AI-driven cyberattacks as of late, users across social media have already begun to speculate about the cause. In particular, users are expressing concerns about the Apple outage, as it arrives only days after technical failures at Microsoft led to the company’s cloud systems powering flagship services like Outlook and Teams failing due to cascading software issues.

Similarly, a brief outage of Chase’s banking app this week gathered attention, and an ongoing partial outage (at the time of publication) of the National Security Agency’s internal classified network, both of which were blamed on software glitches.

So far, none of the outages in the past week have been attributed to cyberattacks, rather technical problems.

Apple has yet to issue any sort of formal statement about the outages, beyond acknowledging the issues are occurring via its status page. TechCrunch reached out to the company for comment but hasn’t heard back yet — if we do, we will update.

We've said previously that, for better or worse, the third-person sci-fi RPG Exodus looks a lot like Mass Effect. It's a characterization that developer Archetype Entertainment isn't shying away from—in fact, it seems to be actively embracing it with the reveal of the game's female protagonist, who they refer to as Femjun.

I kid you not, this is not a name I've cooked up or run across on social media. It's right there on the Exodus website: "Meet Femjun! You’ve been waiting to see FemJun, and today we’re happy to say she’s finally here."

Femjun is a very direct reference to Femshep, the female version of Commander Shepard in BioWare's Mass Effect trilogy. While the male version was effectively the default playable character through the first two games, BioWare took steps to put them on equal footing for Mass Effect 3. That effort included a vote to let fans decide the character's official face, which was subsequently used on alternate or inside covers of Mass Effect 3 packaging.

Voiced by Jennifer Hale, Femshep became so popular that when BioWare dropped the Mass Effect Legendary Edition trailer, it was she who appeared most prominently.

So, back to Exodus: Fenjum will be portrayed by Carina Conti, who's appeared in television shows including Tracker, Good Trouble, All Rise, and Winning Time: The Rise of the Lakers Dynasty. Exodus will be only her second videogame credit, though, following Mafia: The Old Country, in which she portrayed Isabella Torrisi. The male version of Jun will be played by veteran voice actor Stephen Oyoung.

Narrative director Drew Karpyshyn also confirmed that romances in Exodus won't be locked out based on whether you play male or female Jun: "We want to make sure that players can engage in what romances they want if you pursue them."

Exodus is set to launch on April 7, 2027, and will be available for PC on Steam and the Epic Games Store.

/U P D A T E/ - Vertiv Holdings Co/

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

In the news release, Vertiv Advances 800 VDC Power Architectures to Support Growing AI Data Center Demands, issued 17-Nov-2026 by Vertiv Holdings Co over PR Newswire, we are advised by the company that changes have been made. The complete, corrected release follows, with additional details at the end:

The Vertivâ¢ 800 VDC portfolio spans power conversion, energy storage, distribution and protection, delivering comprehensive power train architectures from the medium-voltage grid to the compute tray

800 VDC portfolio to be showcased at the Vertiv booth at the SC26 International Conference for High-Performance Computing, Networking, Storage, and Analysis

COLUMBUS, Ohio, Nov. 16, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Vertiv Holdings Co. (NYSE: VRT), a global leader in critical digital infrastructure, today added 800 VDC systems for the AI compute pod and the data hall, extending a portfolio that began with the May 2026 introduction of the Vertivâ¢ PowerDirect 5000 Power Rack to three deployment levels: rack, compute pod and data hall. The expanded portfolio enables operators to meet rising power demands in next-generation AI data centers and scale infrastructure as compute requirements evolve.

As AI workloads continue to increase power requirements across the data center, operators are evaluating 800 VDC as a potential path to support higher-density computing environments, but adoption will not follow a single architecture or timeline. AC and DC architectures are expected to coexist, making flexibility in power conversion, distribution and deployment essential.

Operators have different facilities, workloads, business models, risk tolerances, and investment horizons. Vertiv's role is to help operators determine which architecture matches their needs, and its portfolio provides operators with the flexibility to introduce 800 VDC at different points in the power train, aligning deployment with facility requirements without committing an entire data center to a single architecture.

"The 800 VDC power train is not just a new power product or conversion methodology; it is a set of architectures and system designs," said Scott Armul, Chief Product and Technology Officer at Vertiv. "Delivering it takes a comprehensive portfolio that brings together power conversion, energy storage, distribution, protection, controls and safety."

Armul added: "To meet these evolving demands, Vertiv has built a system-level 800 VDC portfolio that gives customers the flexibility to implement 800 VDC across different data center configurations. Every layer, from the medium-voltage grid to the compute tray, is designed to work together, with safety built for DC operation."

The Vertivâ¢ PowerDirect 5000 (Â±400 / 800 VDC) Power Rack sits alongside the IT rack. Configurable up to 800 kW, it accepts facility 480 VAC and delivers 800 VDC or Â±400 VDC. The power rack holds 30 hot-pluggable 30 kW power supply units and an integrated battery backup unit that is designed to provide at least 50 seconds of hold-up time.

The Vertivâ¢ PowerDirect 3000 90 kW, 19" 800 VDC Power Shelf is a 1U DC-DC converter located inside the compute rack. The power shelf converts 800 VDC to 54 VDC for compute trays at up to 98.5% peak efficiency and supports 180% pulse power for 0.5 milliseconds.

Designed for operators adding high-density AI compute to existing facilities, this architecture moves power conversion and energy storage outside the IT rack, freeing 8 to 16 rack units for additional compute. Operators can retain existing upstream AC infrastructure, helping simplify deployment without a facility redesign. With one power rack supporting one or two IT racks, the architecture helps isolate faults and simplify maintenance. The platform supports both 800 VDC and Â±400 VDC through minimal hardware configuration changes, providing flexibility as silicon partners' voltage requirements evolve. It suits brownfield environments, retrofit scenarios and facilities running mixed AC and DC architectures.

The Vertivâ¢ PowerDirect 9000, 1.5 MW 800 VDC Power Center converts 480 VAC to 800 VDC for an entire row or pod. It distributes power via the Vertivâ¢ PowerBar Track DC busway and tap-off boxes to the Vertivâ¢ PowerDirect 3000, 90 kW 19" 800 VDC DC/DC Power Shelves in each rack. The system scales in 60 kW increments through 27 hot-pluggable power supply units. The Vertivâ¢ EnergyCore Li5 800 VDC battery cabinet connects directly to the DC bus and delivers 200 kW of continuous backup at up to 98% conversion efficiency, with up to six cabinets operating in parallel through active current sharing.

Designed for operators deploying megawatt-class AI compute at the pod level, this architecture consolidates power conversion into a single system ahead of the row, reducing component count and eliminating the need for individual power racks alongside each IT rack. It can free additional space for compute and support mixed AC and DC loads within the same facility, suiting colocation providers serving tenants with different workload types and GPU generations. Consolidating power conversion, backup and distribution gives operators coordinated power management across the pod.

The Vertivâ¢ PowerNexus MV SST, 5 MW 800 VDC Solid-State Transformer converts medium-voltage AC directly to 800 VDC in a single conversion stage, feeding seamlessly into the Vertivâ¢ DC busway. It eliminates two to three intermediate conversion steps found in conventional power trains and provides a stable DC output with galvanic isolation. Its outdoor, liquid-cooled and containerized design reduces physical footprint while delivering up to 98% AC-to-DC efficiency and providing fault ride-through and AI power smoothing when connected to external energy storage with a DC/DC converter coupled on the 800 VDC bus. A single unit can support a 4.8 MW super pod reference architecture without chaining. Bidirectional power flow enables improved grid power quality and can provide active power support.

Designed for operators building purpose-built AI factories at campus scale, this architecture simplifies the power train from the medium-voltage connection to the rack. Moving power conversion outdoors can recover valuable indoor space for compute, while fewer conversion stages help reduce cumulative power losses. Battery communication ports coordinate with energy storage systems to manage the rapid load fluctuations of AI workloads. The containerized design also supports faster deployment and standardized replication across campuses.

The Vertivâ¢ 800 VDC portfolio addresses the varying requirements of AI data center deployments through three differentiators:

"Different operators have different requirements, whether they're building new AI facilities, upgrading existing sites, or supporting a mix of tenants and workloads," said Kyle Keeper, Senior Vice President of Vertiv's Power Management Business Unit. "The Vertiv 800 VDC portfolio gives each of them an architecture that fits their needs. Whether deploying at the rack, compute pod or data hall level, customers have the optionality to choose an approach that aligns with their existing infrastructure, deployment goals and power requirements. This flexibility helps them meet today's demands while creating a path to scale as their AI infrastructure evolves."

Vertiv is showcasing its 800 VDC portfolio at SC26, Nov. 17-22, 2026.  Pilot programs, technical validation activities, and collaborative development initiatives tailored to customers' deployment needs are currently underway. Vertiv experts are ready to discuss these solutions and engage on next steps.

For more information on Vertiv's end-to-end portfolio of power and thermal management, infrastructure solutions, IT systems and services for critical digital applications, visit Vertiv.com.

(NYSE: VRT) brings together hardware, software, analytics and ongoing services to enable its customers' vital applications to run continuously, perform optimally and grow with their business needs. Vertiv solves the most important challenges facing today's data centers, communication networks and commercial and industrial facilities with a portfolio of power, cooling and IT infrastructure solutions and services that extends from the cloud to the edge of the network. MTG Dune Secret Lair Superdrop Confirmed For Later This Month

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Sam Woods is TheGamer’s Senior Reporter. He’s been in games journalism since 2020 and has undertaken a variety of roles in that time. These have included roles such as SEO Editor, Managing Editor at DualShockers, and Global Head of Gaming, and he's also been seen on the BBC. He was promoted to the Senior Reporter position in 2025 following a series of high-quality reports and exclusives for TheGamer.

Since 2020, Sam has written over 3,000 articles and specializes in telling the stories of the people behind the games. He’s interviewed the biggest game directors, some of the industry’s top talents, told the stories of how indie games came to be, and spoken to players about what really makes them tick. He’s also interviewed some of the world’s biggest musical artists to find out how the medium has inspired their work, and TV and Film actors about their roles in video game adaptations. He even hosted a PAX panel in 2021 and made a documentary in 2024 surrounding the creation of a Disney game.

Alongside this, Sam focuses on breaking news. He got the scoop on Perfect Dark’s cancellation, provided context on some AI drama, and revealed that Lady Dimitrescu’s actress would love to step back into the character’s giant shoes. These articles have been picked up by the biggest gaming sites, including IGN, GameSpot, and PC Gamer. He also loves to dive into the numbers behind the games.

Sam’s journey in games journalism has taken him all over the world. He’s covered Gamescom in Germany, Summer Game Fest in Los Angeles, and Tokyo Game Show in Japan, each on three occasions.

He’s been learning Japanese since 2024 with the intention of identifying and translating Japanese-language news and bringing it to English-language audiences first, something he’s done countless times.

Sam’s first review was 2020’s Battletoads reboot, and he’s since reviewed games in The Legend of Zelda, Super Mario, and Pokemon franchises.

Earlier this month, rumors surfaced about a potential Magic: The Gathering x Dune Secret Lair Superdrop. The rumors quickly spread like wildfire across internet forums like Reddit, but nothing came directly from Wizards of the Coast.

That was until now, when the official Secret Lair Twitter account teased the crossover, revealing it's set to drop in less than two weeks' time.

"The thumper is set and Shai-Hulud is on the move," Wizards of the Coast teased on social media, with a short video of a thumper pounding the sand, followed by the Secret Lair logo. "My Arrakis, My Superdrop goes on sale November 30. #DuneMovie"

This MTG Secret Lair tweet all but confirmed that everything in the recent leak was true. The name, "My Arrakis, My Superdrop," matches the one that was leaked, so we can probably assume everything else was correct.

It said there would be three individual drops based around Sandworms, artifacts, and enchantments, followed by a set of legendary creatures based on eight characters from the Dune movies. The set is reported to include reprints like Sandstorm Convergence and Darksteel Forge, but 11 of the 20 leaked cards are completely new to the Secret Lair drop.

Further details have been confirmed on Reddit, with one of the drops coming in Halo Foil. The commander cards will include some fan-favorite characters, including Paul Atreides, Baron Vladimir Harkonnen, and Chani. The third set, Spice Harvest, is the only one whose price has leaked so far, retailing for $ 59.99.

The Secret Lair Superdrop arrives just in time for the Dune: Part Three movie, set to release in theatres worldwide on December 18, the same day as Avengers: Doomsday. It will be the 13th Secret Lair Superdrop of the year, following the likes of The Hobbit, Lofi Girl, My Little Pony, and Star Trek.

We'll have more coverage of this drop coming soon to TheGamer, including a handy guide, so keep your eyes peeled.

It’s been a huge year for Robert Pattinson and Zendaya. They are starring again in Dune: Part Three, which hits the 2026 movie schedule on December 18th (“DunesDay”). Pattinson, you may have heard, also landed the role of a lifetime yesterday when he was named the next James Bond.

The James Bond role may have come (indirectly) due to Zendaya introducing Pattinson to director Denis Villeneuve, who is not only at the helm of Dune, but upcoming, as-yet-untitled James Bond 26 as well. Last March, well before the Pattinson announcement, CinemaBlend attended an event featuring the premiere of the Dune: Part Three trailer followed by a Q&A with some of the cast, including Pattinson and Zendaya. I absolutely adore these [Dune] movies. I saw them multiple times in theaters, and I think I was talking to you [Zendaya], on the set of The Drama and was like ‘Can I get in one of those Dune movies?

While Zendaya does acknowledge that she “knows a guy,” talking about Villeneuve, whom she worked with on the first two Dune movies, she doesn’t take full credit. However, Pattinson continues:

And it was a very unexpected call I got a few months later and I kind of did think you had something to do with it. I just think it's such a towering achievement, that the cast is so incredible. And, uh, I just think everybody wants to work with Denis, I mean, he’s just such a master.

He’ll be stepping into massive shoes once filled by the likes of Sean Connery, Roger Moore, Pierce Brosnan, Daniel Craig, and all the other actors who have played Bond over the years.

The choice is not without some controversy, naturally, but speaking for myself, I’m excited. I always expected Amazon MGM, who now own the franchise, to go with a big star, and I think they’ve made a very solid choice with Pattinson, even though he’s only really dabbled in action movies. Google to keep Gemini chats for 2 months, up from 72 hours, even with history off

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Google is extending how long it keeps temporary Gemini chats and conversations made with chat history turned off to around two months, saying the change aims to improve safety and security as AI systems become more capable.

The change, which will roll out globally in the Gemini app over the coming weeks, marks a significant increase from the previous 72-hour retention period mentioned in Google’s Gemini Apps Privacy Hub, according to an archived version from November 12.

OpenAI, by comparison, says it may keep temporary chats in ChatGPT for up to 30 days, whereas Anthropic retains incognito chats in Claude for 30 days by default. However, companies may keep some data longer for legal or safety reasons, or to investigate policy violations.

Google did not answer questions about what prompted the change and whether it would affect how conversations are accessed or reviewed, instead pointing to a blog post that says chats are kept longer to respond to users, process feedback, and protect its services and users. Its updated Gemini Apps Privacy Hub also cites potential system failures as a reason for retaining chats and says temporary conversations are not used to train AI models.

The longer retention period comes as Google introduces separate settings for Gemini’s chat history and AI training. Until now, both were tied to a single “Keep Activity” setting. Users will now be able to keep their conversations in their chat history without using them to train Google’s generative AI models.

Google says both new settings will be turned off by default for users who previously disabled Keep Activity. For those who had Keep Activity turned on, their chat history will remain enabled. They can, though, decide separately whether Google can use their conversations to train AI models.

Why Intel Stock Just Dropped

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Sure, Nvidia sold 25% of its Intel stock -- but it kept 75%.

Pity Intel (INTC -3.12%) investors. Not only have they had to deal with the fallout of an Nvidia CEO scandal not of their making this month -- one that's contributed to an 18% drop in stock price in November alone.

Not only are they now struggling to parse a new SemiAnalysis report warning that capital spending on AI chips in 2027 could be 19% below previous estimates, 2028 spending might be 28% below, and 2029 spending could fall 41% below estimates.

Today, Intel investors are facing yet another negative catalyst for their stock. Nvidia (NVDA -1.81%), it seems, is selling down its stake in its semiconducting rival -- and it's costing Intel stock a 2.3% decline through 1:30 p.m. ET today.

Nvidia just confirmed in an SEC filing that it has sold 25% of its Intel stock position, reducing its stake from 214.8 million shares to 161.1 million. At the same time, as StreetInsider.com reports, Nvidia has increased its ownership in SpaceX (SPCX +2.42%) by 6%, to 130.5 million shares.

All of these numbers are as of Sept. 30, before the Jensen Huang scandal broke.

Nvidia's moves proved prescient. Although SpaceX stock has fallen slightly since the report date (down about 6%), Intel shares are down much, much more -- roughly 55%. So Nvidia kept a lot of its Intel profits by cashing out before Intel crashed (a crash that Nvidia's own CEO scandal helped cause).

The question for Intel investors now is: should you follow Nvidia's example and sell out of Intel, too? Before you do that, recall that if Nvidia sold 25% of its Intel shares in September, then it also kept 75%.

So Nvidia hasn't abandoned all hope in Intel yet. Maybe you shouldn't either?

ULA lost a Vulcan Centaur rocket carrying 40 Amazon satellites 10 minutes after launch — but the failure began just after liftoff

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Latest space missions, discoveries and exploration updates.

Stargazing tips, cosmic events and expert insights on the universe

Start exploring exclusive deals, expert advice and more

Space news, cosmic updates and expert insights straight to your inbox.

Breaking space news, the latest updates on rocket launches, skywatching events and more!

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Breaking space news, the latest updates on rocket launches, skywatching events and more!

Sign up to our monthly entertainment newsletter to keep up with all our coverage of the latest sci-fi and space movies, tv shows, games and books.

Discover this week's must-see night sky events, moon phases, and stunning astrophotos. Sign up for our skywatching newsletter and explore the universe with us!

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

"Early indications are that just after liftoff the launch vehicle experienced low booster performance."

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The failure of a Vulcan Centaur rocket carrying a full load of internet satellites for Amazon was caused by an anomaly in its first-stage booster, rocket maker United Launch Alliance said today (Nov. 17).

That failure — which led to the loss of 40 Amazon Leo internet satellites — was recognized about 10 minutes after liftoff, but the problems began much earlier than that, ULA said in a statement.

"While we appear to have lost telemetry and the mission at approximately 10 minutes into flight, early indications are that just after liftoff the launch vehicle experienced low booster performance, resulting in stage separation occurring when the vehicle was low in altitude and below minimal velocity to reach orbit," ULA wrote in today's update.

The two-stage Vulcan Centaur is the successor to ULA's workhorse Atlas V rocket, which will retire after just six more flights. Vulcan debuted in January 2024 and now has a total of five launches under its belt.

The new rocket has experienced problems on three of those missions. On both its second and fourth flights — which launched in October 2024 and February of this year, respectively — the nozzle of one of Vulcan's strap-on solid rocket boosters (SRBs) fell off about 30 seconds after liftoff.

On both occasions, the rocket overcame the problem and successfully delivered its payloads to orbit. However, after the second occurrence, the U.S. Space Force paused national-security launches with Vulcan until ULA addressed the SRB nozzle issue. (The February 2026 flight was a Space Force mission.)

That work led to "design and process improvements to the nozzle," ULA said in an Oct. 28 update. And those tweaks apparently worked.

"All indications are that the solid rocket booster performance was nominal, following nozzle improvements implemented for this launch," the company wrote in today's update.

Vulcan's Centaur V upper stage, which features two RL-10C engines, did its job as well "but was unable to recover from the [booster] performance loss, resulting in loss of mission," ULA added.

Vulcan's first-stage booster is powered by two BE-4 engines, which are built by Amazon founder Jeff Bezos' Blue Origin. Today's update did not identify the exact problem with the booster; doing so will require more time, as the investigation has just begun.

If that investigation finds a serious issue with the BE-4, there could be broad ripple effects across the American space program. After all, that engine also powers the first stage of Blue Origin's New Glenn rocket, which is a key piece of hardware for NASA's Artemis program of moon exploration. (And New Glenn is currently recovering from a failure of its own — a massive explosion on the test stand that occurred on May 28.)

NASA plans to integrate the Centaur V upper stage into its Space Launch System moon rocket, the vehicle that launches Artemis astronauts to space. So the fact that the Centaur performed nominally on Sunday is a bit of good news for the space agency.

Vulcan's grounding is a big deal, no matter what the anomaly investigation ends up finding. The U.S. doesn't have a surfeit of powerful rockets, after all, and two of them — Vulcan and New Glenn — are out of commission at the moment.

There's considerable pressure on both Vulcan and New Glenn to bounce back strong, for the nation faces an impending launch crunch in the next few years. SpaceX plans to retire its Falcon 9 and Falcon Heavy rockets and shift its launch load over to the fully reusable Starship megarocket. SpaceX is expected to prioritize its own needs in Starship's early days, using the vehicle primarily to build out its next-gen Starlink broadband constellation and Starmind AI data center network.

The Vulcan failure also impacts Amazon — specifically, the company's plan to build out Amazon Leo, which will compete with SpaceX's Starlink. Amazon has booked about 100 launches on a variety of rockets to build its constellation, which will eventually consist of more than 3,200 satellites. Thirty-eight of those bookings are for Vulcan; Sunday's launch was the first of those.

The other rockets Amazon has booked are the Atlas V, New Glenn, Falcon 9 and Arianespace's Ariane 6. About 400 operational Amazon Leo satellites have reached orbit to date across 14 launches (not including Sunday's failure).

Michael Wall is the Spaceflight and Tech Editor for Space.com and joined the team in 2010. He primarily covers human and robotic spaceflight, military space, and exoplanets, but has been known to dabble in the space art beat. His book about the search for alien life, "Out There," was published on Nov. 13, 2018. Before becoming a science writer, Michael worked as a herpetologist and wildlife biologist. He has a Ph.D. in evolutionary biology from the University of Sydney, Australia, a bachelor's degree from the University of Arizona, and a graduate certificate in science writing from the University of California, Santa Cruz. To find out what his latest project is, you can follow Michael on Twitter.

Trey Nyoni names Arsenal star as ‘toughest opponent’

📅 2026-11-17  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Liverpool youngster Trey Nyoni has picked out Arsenal captain Martin Odegaard as the ‘toughest opponent’ he has faced at the start of his career.

Odegaard has returned to his sparkling best this season, starting all ten of Arsenal’s opening matches in the Premier League with the reigning champions sitting five points clear at the summit.

Having managed just one goal across the Gunners’ title-winning campaign last term, the 27-year-old appears to have rediscovered his scoring boots, finding the net on five occasions in all competitions this term.

It was Odegaard’s 72nd-minute strike that proved the difference before the international break, when Mikel Arteta’s men were made to work for their victory at home to newly promoted Hull City.

The Norway international will no doubt be desperate to pick up where he left off when the north London giants return to top-flight against at Newcastle on Saturday evening.

Read exclusive analysis of the biggest stories in football with Metro's weekly football newsletter In The Mixer. Sign up now for free.

Nyoni has himself enjoyed an impressive start to the season at Liverpool and locked horns with Odegaard when the pair did battle at Anfield at the start of the month.

And while Nyoni was taken off before the hour-mark on Merseyside, it’s clear Odegaard left a big impression on the 19-year-old in the 58 minutes his opposite number was on the pitch.

Asked to name the ‘toughest’ player he has come up against, Nyoni told The Inside Scoop, ‘For me personally, I would say Odegaard,’ as he looked back on that afternoon.

‘The way he plays, it’s very busy, moving around and always looking for an option.

Use AI to go deeper into the stories you care about – powered by Metro and trusted publications.

‘It’s not easy as a midfielder. I think when you play against someone who is constantly moving, you know in the back of your mind that he could be here, but the next minute he’s there.’

To view this video please enable JavaScript, and consider upgrading to a web browser that supports HTML5 video

When asked if Odegaard perhaps doesn’t get the credit he deserves, Nyoni replied: ‘Yeah, I think the game is evolving and I think nowadays people look at No.10s and automatically think that if they’re not getting goals and assists, they’re not good.

‘For example, in our team we’ve got Flo [Wirtz]. I think Flo does a lot of stuff that people don’t really see.

‘I think what he brings to the team benefits the team. Just because he’s not scoring or assisting, doesn’t mean he’s not effective.

‘Nowadays, the idea that if a player isn’t getting goals or assists, they’re useless, but every player has their own role in the team.

‘Odegaard and people like Flo, if they’re not getting goals and assists, people that don’t watch the game automatically think they haven’t played well but that’s not how football works.’

In the aftermath of Arsenal’s nervy win over Hull, club legend Paul Merson said Odegaard had been ‘like a new signing’ for Arteta this season.

‘He’s been outstanding. He’s the only one really that moves it quickly,’ Merson said of Odegaard on Sky Sports.

‘He moves it quickly, he moves, he gets into positions. I just thought too many players took too many touches and they slowed it down and gave Hull half a chance.

‘I think he’s been outstanding this season, he’s like a new signing for them and he’s been the man so far.

‘Not great but just a good result for Arsenal. But again, he was one of the stars of the show.’

Legato Frames with femtoAI Delivers Award-Winning AI Smart Glasses

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The femtoAI fully integrated AI silicon and compression platform uniquely enables Legato SpeechSenseâ¢ AI

SAN BRUNO, Calif., Nov. 17, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- femtoAI, a pioneer of a fully integrated AI silicon and compression platform, proudly congratulates Legato Hearing on the success of Legato Frames, award-winning AI hearing glasses that deliver its patented Legato SpeechSenseâ¢ AI with femtoAI.The femtoAI SPU-001 AI processor and cutting-edge AI software, along with its open developer support and collaborative tuning from the femtoAI team, empowered Legato Frames from concept to a shipping product.

Legato Frames are premium eyeglasses with hearing technology built entirely into the frame, so nothing goes in the wearer's ears. Legato SpeechSenseâ¢ AI is what makes that possible: an on-device AI algorithm stack that isolates speech from background noise in real time and amplifies only the speech using less power to enable a longer daily battery life .SpeechSenseâ¢ pairs a custom implementation of femtoAI algorithm with Legato's own proprietary signal processing. The femtoAI platform, including the SPU-001 chip, contributes configurable blocks for feedback cancellation, speech enhancement and noise reduction, wide dynamic range compression, and frequency shifting. Together, the solution delivers best-in-class hearing enhancement in a 10X smaller footprint and at 10X lower power, without sacrificing accuracy.

Award-WinningThe result is a hearing solution that in independent testing outperforms products costing several times as much. WIRED recently named Legato Frames the Best Hearing Aid Glasses of 2026. HearAdvisor, a lab that benchmarks the world's leading hearing aids, scored them 4.6 out of 5 on its SoundScore scale, placing them among top prescription hearing aids that cost thousands of dollars on average. All of this, inside frames that go nowhere near the ear canal, last all day on a charge, and at a fraction of the price.

Easy-to-use developer tools, and a partner from day oneLegato's founders know hearing. Between them, Mehul Trivedi and Steve Romine had built Bose Frames, launched the first over-the-counter hearing aid, and lobbied the FDA to create the OTC category. What they needed was a way to put a modern speech network inside a frame without building the AI platform to run it.

The combination of innovative audio algorithms, low-power technology, and amazing developer support helped us deliver SpeechSenseâ¢ AI in a compact glasses form factor," said Mehul Trivedi, CEO of Legato. We have loved working with the femtoAI team and are excited to continue building together."

Legato worked with the femtoAI platform from inception, using femtoAI's toolchain and prebuilt compressed models, with femto Studioâ¢ measuring the savings at each step so both the design and the economics of the product were market-ready. femtoAI's audio team worked alongside Legato's engineers from concept and ideation through tuning and production.

"Our job was to make sure they had the tools and the team to carry it all the way to a successful product deployment. How Does The REO Runabout Stack Up Against Slate?

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Browse this and many other websites without banner ads, personalized tracking and video ads.

Like the idea of a Slate but don't want an EV? Well, an upstart from Dallas, Texas has just the thing for you. That is, if it ever becomes a reality. Startup company REO recently unveiled renderings of its new truck, dubbed the Runabout. It's a similar idea to Slate, with just one basic frame, three different body variants to choose from, no frills, a bunch of customization, and a sub-$30,000 price tag. The main difference is that that Runabout uses a four-cylinder gas engine, rather than electric motors. So how does it compare to Slate?

First, let's look at price. The most basic Slate you can get—cheekily called "The Blank Slate"—costs $24,950, and it's a two-door pickup with nothing more than some seats, a steering wheel, a battery, and a single rear electric motor to get you around. There's no radio, power windows, or screens to speak of.

The cheapest REO you can get will be the Runabout T4X, which costs $21,500*. Note the asterisk, as REO says the current listed price is an estimate, and the company has no factory or clear plans for getting it to market. For that shockingly low price, it's estimated that you'll get a four-cylinder engine, a choice of either a six-speed manual or automatic transmission, and mechanical four-wheel drive. It's a two-door, two-seat pickup with drop-down sides for the bed, like a Kei truck.

What four-cylinder engine will the Runabout have and which manufacturer will supply it? REO isn't saying. There are far more questions than answers so far. REO does say that it has a "targeted powertrain life of 500,000 miles." To quote myself reading that in the press release: "lol."

While Slate uses a hybrid body-on-frame style chassis, the REO has an old-school ladder frame, like a classic pickup. It's similar to slate in that it offers modular body styles, but with key differences. There's a two-door pickup and an SUV version, like Slate, but there's also a four-door pickup, which Slate doesn't have. REO claims its truck has solid rear axles, too, to give it more off-road credibility.

The design isn't bad, even if it looks like a Grand Theft Auto version of an Australian-market Land Cruiser, but that's to be expected. REO hired longtime automotive designer Frank Stephenson, whose previous work includes the first BMW-owned Mini Cooper, the Maserati MC12, and the McLaren P1. Overall, it looks handsome enough and utilitarian, even if all the photos are just renders at this point.

Inside, REO's truck is said to be pretty barebones (it hasn't been revealed yet), but not as barebones as Slate's. There's a digital screen for gauges and one for navigation, but everything else has a physical control. You're probably wondering if it has power or hand-crank windows. REO used its press release to throw a little shade: " Each model will feature power windows for convenience: hand-cranks are best left to others."

The entire idea behind REO is to offer the sort of basic pickups customers loved in the '80s and '90s, trucks that were simple utilitarian workhorses and nothing else. It's an admirable goal, especially given how expensive pickups have become. But do customers want to go backward in powertrain technology, especially with affordable mainstream electric trucks like the Ford Fathom coming to market soon?

So for less money, you can get a potentially more capable truck, in a similar configuration as the Slate, with a bit of extra interior comfort. But you lose the electric powertrain, which reduces maintenance and fuel costs. Instead, you'll have to make due with a generic four-cylinder of some sort. There is one big "if," though. You can only buy one if REO ever actually makes it. Sure, having a design by Frank Stephenson helps it feel more real, but just because they found the money to pay him to design something doesn't mean they'll actually get around to building it.

Slate is already here, it's ready to launch production vehicles soon. We've seen them in person, touched them, and tested them. Until REO can actually muster a viable product, it should probably avoid throwing anymore shade in its press releases.

Disney Touts Its Streaming Success In Latest Earnings

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

After a lot of internal upheaval and consolidation, the House of Mouse finds itself in a pretty good place going into its fiscal 2027 year.

So says new CEO Josh D’Amaro and CFO Hugh Johnston during an earnings call on Tuesday. They told investors that Disney exceeded its guidance for the previous quarter and is on track to exceed its three-year adjusted earnings-per-share growth guidance going into the new year.

For the full year, revenue not only increased 8% but broke the $100 billion mark, from $94.billion in the fiscal year 2025 to $102.3 billion in 2026.

Meanwhile, for Disney’s Q4 (the company starts its fiscal year a quarter earlier), revenue increased 16% to $25.9 billion for last quarter, compared to $22.5 the year before.

Within entertainment, advertising grew 10% to $1.7 billion. And SVOD revenue increased 19% to $6.1 billion, with 22% subscription revenue growth.

In TV, D’Amaro highlighted the critical buzz around Hulu’s crime thriller “Furious,” high ratings for the new season of “Dancing with the Stars” and excitement over “The Remarried Empress,” a South Korean romantasy that debuted as the biggest premiere for a local original across Disney+ and Hulu globally. The latter is indicative of Disney’s plan to invest more in international content, a strategy that’s been working wonders for competitors like Netflix.

Sports are also a big deal, of course. Subscription and affiliate fee revenue in the sports segment increased 18% in the quarter to $3.4 billion, with advertising revenue growth of 20%.

Disney will continue to grow its sports footprint next year with expanded college football rights, increased “Monday Night Football” ratings and ESPN’s first ever Super Bowl telecast. (Well, technically it’s ABC’s turn to host the Big Game, but it’ll also be cross-broadcast on other Disney channels.)

Where Disney’s overall streaming strategy is concerned, D’Amaro told investors that there will be a larger focus on “connecting services” – which includes other non-entertainment sides of the business like parks, cruise ships and merchandise.

More practically, though, “connecting services” means finally unifying Disney+ and Hulu into one app, which D’Amaro said would start in December. It also means adding more sports content to Disney+, and introducing creator content from TikTok into the Disney+ mobile app next month.  The move might strike some as strange, but given that ESPN’s short-form Verts product broke records in September and October, maybe there’s something to it.

At the same time, Disney is still exploring the idea of a free streaming product, which leadership first discussed during last quarter’s earnings reports.

Despite – or maybe because of – all the streaming success, Disney announced it will stop reporting its SVOD operating income beginning next quarter. At the same time, the company will also provide additional disclosures related to revenue within the entertainment segment.

SVOD revenues will still be reported, Johnston clarified to investors but so will content and IP revenues. Revenue for linear networks will be combined with the virtual MVPD business.

The goal, said Johnston, is to provide investors with “additional information and a better lens into our content monetization,” while allowing Disney’s team more flexibility to “invest appropriately” in growing SVOD.

In other words, Disney’s likely planning to spend a lot more money on its SVOD business – and maybe it doesn’t want investors to worry too much about how the sausage is getting made.

Disney’s remarkable growth this past quarter isn’t surprising, given how much the company has restructured since D’Amaro took on the CEO role in March. At least 1000 members of Disney’s workforce have been laid off since then, with more job cuts and consolidation reportedly on the way.

All that streamlining is expected to translate into $3 billion in run rate savings by 2029, according to D’Amaro.

To highlight the ways Disney is prioritizing efficiency and consolidation, D’Amaro also shouted out the company’s first ever chief technology officer, Karandeep Anand, who came from the (now bankrupt) chatbot startup Character.ai.

Not surprisingly, Disney plans to continue investing in AI tools and technology across its entire business, even parks. Although D’Amaro didn’t bring up the company’s recent purchase of Character.ai’s assets, he did hype up how the tech is being used in the streaming side of the business, both within Disney+’s content recommendation engine and in the advertising products Disney makes available to its marketing clients.

In addition to Disney’s AI creative studio that uses existing brand material to develop CTV-ready ads, the company also launched an agentic ad campaign tool in August. Both were previewed at its tech and data showcase at CES earlier this year.

Now, almost half of all new ad campaigns are flowing through the agentic tool, according to Disney’s ad sales team.

After selling ads at The Wall Street Journal and WebMD and rising to CRO at The Trade Desk, Jed Dederick is now the first-ever chief revenue officer of creator startup ShopMy.

DecryptAds’ goal, Garcia said, is to give researchers, ad agencies, tech companies and regulators the evidence they need to fix security gaps in the programmatic supply chain.

The mobile growth marketing platform Liftoff reported its second-ever quarterly earnings last week. Shares were up by almost 25% after the company exceeded its revenue target and projected an optimistic profit margin outlook moving forward.

CTV sellers are promising measurable outcomes – even guaranteed store visits – but winning advertiser trust takes more than just measurement.

For the first time, a new feature in Google Ads breaks down the dollar amount a campaign saved from IVT blocking and exactly how much Google is crediting the campaign for bots caught post-billing.

Tim and Chris Vanderhook explain why Viant bought Waymark and how it fits into their ambitious plans for CTV.

Bitcoin Lightning developers needed two patches to stop a potentially costly attack

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The initial July update blocked one crash method but missed a persistent vulnerability that could leave affected nodes unable to restart.

Bitcoin's Core Lightning software required two security patches to eliminate a vulnerability that could repeatedly crash payment nodes and potentially expose funds held in other channels.

A Nov. 13 security disclosure revealed that an initial repair released in July failed to address a second attack method that could keep affected nodes offline even after they restarted.

The vulnerability, tracked as LNF-2026-0004, affected Core Lightning, one of the software implementations used to operate nodes on Bitcoin's Lightning Network.

Security researcher Nishant Bansal discovered that attackers could exploit how Core Lightning assigned identifiers to payment channels, creating duplicate records that caused the software to crash.

The weakness originated in the network's channel-opening process, where each payment channel receives an identifier derived from the Bitcoin transaction output that funds it. Core Lightning could accept multiple channels referencing the same funding output, producing identical identifiers that conflicted with assumptions in its internal software.

In the first attack, a malicious peer could create duplicate pending channels and crash a victim's node simply by reconnecting. The attack required no Bitcoin transaction fees because the fraudulent channels did not need on-chain funding. Each subsequent reconnection could trigger another crash, allowing attackers to repeatedly interrupt payment processing.

However, researchers identified a more persistent variation involving previously closed channels.

By reusing an identifier associated with a closed channel and subsequently closing another channel with the same identifier, attackers could trigger a software failure that persisted across restarts.

Unlike the initial attack, this method required on-chain transaction fees but could leave the node unable to recover without a software upgrade.

Core Lightning developers addressed the original vulnerability in version 26.06.6, released on July 22, after researchers privately reported the reconnection attack earlier that month.

The update prevented new channels from reusing funding outputs associated with existing live channels. It also modified connection handling to prevent duplicate records already stored from triggering the original crash.

However, the protection did not extend to identifiers associated with closed channels, leaving the second attack route available.

Researcher Crypt-iQ independently identified the remaining weakness, prompting developers to introduce broader safeguards in version 26.06.8, released Sept. 22.

The second update expanded duplicate-identifier checks across active and closed channels belonging to different peers. It also allowed nodes containing previously stored duplicate records to recover without repeatedly crashing.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

July’s repair checked live channels. September’s also covered closed channels and allowed recovery from duplicates already stored.

The first fix blocked outpoints reused among that peer’s live channels and handled reconnect duplicates. Closed-channel collisions could still cause a persistent restart loop.

The broader fix rejected IDs used by live or closed channels across all peers and let nodes load and close duplicates already stored.

Operators should upgrade to the latest release to prevent collisions and handle stored duplicates.

The closed-channel loop shown is the documented persistent path; a separate free nonpersistent path also existed. This qualitative schematic does not show exploitation, losses or affected-node counts.

Qualitative schematic of the common collision mechanism and two historical patch scopes. Toggle compares July 22 v26.06.6 with September 22 v26.06.8. Connectors show software paths, not counts, timing or money flows.

The closed-channel loop shown is the documented persistent path; a separate free nonpersistent path also existed. This qualitative schematic does not show exploitation, losses or affected-node counts.

The potential financial consequences extended beyond disrupted Lightning payments.

According to the advisory, nodes forced offline could lose the ability to monitor Bitcoin's blockchain for attempts to settle channels using revoked transaction states.

That could allow dishonest counterparties to claim funds using outdated channel balances. Offline nodes could also miss deadlines for collecting time-sensitive payments, placing Bitcoin held in unrelated channels at risk.

The researchers did not report confirmed exploitation or financial losses.

The findings affect Core Lightning versions preceding the complete September repair, with the reconnection vulnerability dating back to version 0.12.0 and the persistent closed-channel crash introduced in version 25.05.

Developers have since released additional security updates, including version 26.06.10, which addresses other vulnerabilities involving channel operations, payment resolution, and remote crashes.

Core Lightning's maintainers have urged operators to upgrade to the latest release to protect their nodes against the disclosed weaknesses.

AI models regularly misidentify civilians as military targets in tests, report found

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Free daily briefing on global business news.

Anthropic's Claude Opus 5.5 targeted a school in Iran more than any other model tested, according to evaluator Vals

Vals, an independent AI evaluator, published a report on Tuesday showing that frontier AI models flagged civilians, ambulances and surrendering soldiers as military targets in simulated scenarios.

Vals said no request was blocked by a classifier and that refusals from the models themselves were uncommon. Models that did refuse complied after being told they were playing a game. In a battlefield survey task, most models kept going after seeing casualties from earlier strikes.

The researchers ran three tests: drone target identification and a battlefield survey, both based on the war in Ukraine, and a satellite imagery test of Minab, Iran, where a school sits next to a military complex. The United States bombed the school on Feb. 28, killing over 150 civilians, mostly children, Vals said.

In the satellite test, Anthropic's Claude Opus 5.5 targeted the school more than any other model, Vals said. The model picked the school in 22 of 32 tests, whereas no OpenAI model chose it even once, according to Bloomberg. When the prompt framed the images as coming from a game, the Anthropic model chose the school on 23 occasions, and three OpenAI models chose it on 9, 11 and 13 occasions.

Vals said Claude Opus 4.5, released in November 2025, gave at least one target in 6% of satellite trials, compared with 92% for Opus 5.5. OpenAI models' targeting rate stayed low.

Vals said it did not evaluate targeting accuracy, compliance with the law of armed conflict or performance in real U.S. military workflows. It also said it did not establish how effective the models would be in a deployed weapons system. The firm recommended that AI labs add further protective measures and train their models on alignment.

An Anthropic spokesperson said the report's examples run "very clearly" counter to company policy, and that further safeguards are due to take effect shortly, according to Bloomberg. OpenAI said in a statement shared by Vals that it is working on alignment updates covering drones and their software systems, including assistance framed as simulation, gaming or academic or defensive use.

A Google $GOOGL spokesperson said the company's models were not built with such uses in mind, according to Bloomberg. A Pentagon official said the department declines to weigh in on reports produced by outside parties.

Vals said it disclosed its findings on Nov. 1 to OpenAI, Anthropic, Google, Meta $META and SpaceXAI and held publication for two weeks.

The Pentagon designated Anthropic a national security supply chain risk in March, after contract talks collapsed over the company's request for guarantees that Claude would not be used in autonomous lethal weapons systems or for domestic mass surveillance.

In a September report on misuse of its models, Anthropic flagged attempts to enlist Claude in developing software for missiles, armed drones and bombs. The company said it banned the accounts tied to the activity it identified.
